--- PDF 1 PAGE 2 ---
METHOD 2 FORMULA  
text 
Step 1: Scale Factor = max(|sample|) + ε 
Step 2: Tanh Transformation: T(x) = tanh(x / Scale)  
Step 3: Manhattan Distance: d = Σ|T(S) - T(C)|  
Step 4: IDW Prediction: Predicted = Σ(w_i × Value_i) / Σ(w_i)  
         where w_i = 1/(d_i + ε), power = 2.0

METHOD 3: KNN RATIO FUSION  
COMPLETE CALCULATION FOR PHOSPHORUS (P)  
 
INPUT DATA - Sample BR -7 (Sample Index 21)  
Spectral Data (21 features):  
1159.21, 208.78, 998.73, 281.32, 468.92, 923.83, 386.57, 469.43, 338.56, 94.0, 41.0, 15.0, 572.86, 
183.57, 68.98, 46.0, 110.09, 356.73, 86.629, 3.611, 2.644  
Actual P Value = 142.85  
 
METHOD 3 FORMULA  
text 
Step 1: Compute pairwise ratios between adjacent bands  
        Ratio = signal[i] / signal[j] for j = i+1, i+2  
Step 2: Calculate average ratio for each spectrum  
Step 3: Manhattan Distance = |sample_ratio - cal_ratio|  
Step 4: IDW Prediction with power = 2.0  
        Predicted = Σ(w_i × Value_i) / Σ(w_i)  
        where w_i = 1/(d_i + ε)

METHOD 4: SPECTRAL NUTRIENT INDEX  
COMPLETE CALCULATION FOR POTASSIUM (K)  
 
INPUT DATA - Sample BR -7 (Sample Index 21)  
Spectral Data (21 features):  
1159.21, 208.78, 998.73, 281.32, 468.92, 923.83, 386.57, 469.43, 338.56, 94.0, 41.0, 15.0, 
572.86, 183.57, 68.98, 46.0, 110.09, 356.73, 86.629, 3.611, 2.644  
Actual K Value = 364.74  
METHOD 4 FORMULA  
text 
Step 1: Calculate spread = |MAX - MIN| + ε 
Step 2: Calculate weights = spread / sum(spread)  
Step 3: Calculate index = Σ(spectrum × weights)  
Step 4: Safe interpolation:  
        t = (sample_index - min_index) / (max_index - min_index)  
        t = clip(t, 0, 1)  
        Predicted = LOW + t × (HIGH - LOW)

METHOD 6: UNIFIED ADAPTIVE  
COMPLETE CALCULATION FOR CALCIUM (Ca)  
INPUT DATA - Sample BR -7 (Sample Index 21)  
Spectral Data (21 features):

572.86, 183.57, 68.98, 46.0, 110.09, 356.73, 86.629, 3.611, 2.644  
Actual Ca Value = 9.85635  
METHOD 6 FORMULA  
Step 1: Calculate local interpolation (z_local):  
        For each band i:  
            t_i = (sample[i] - min[i]) / (max[i] - min[i])  
            z_i = LOW + t_i  × (HIGH - LOW)  
            w_i = √(spread[i])  
        z_local = Σ(w_i × z_i) / Σ(w_i)  
Step 2: Calculate global IDW prediction (z_idw):  
        d_MIN = Euclidean(sample, min)  
        d_MID = Euclidean(sample, mid)  
        d_MAX = Euclidean(sample, max)  
        z_idw = IDW(d_MIN, d_MID, d_MAX, LOW, MID, HIGH)  
Step 3: Ensemble:  
        Predicted = 0.4 × z_local + 0.6 × z_idw

METHOD 8: WEIGHTED PROJECTION  
COMPLETE CALCULATION FOR MAGNESIUM (Mg)  
 
INPUT DATA - Sample BR -7 (Sample Index 21)  
Spectral Data (21 features):  
1159.21, 208.78, 998.73, 281.32, 468.92, 923.83, 386.57, 469.43, 338.56, 94.0, 41.0, 15.0,

Actual Mg Value = 5.29432  
--- PDF 1 PAGE 38 ---
METHOD 8 FORMULA  
text 
Step 1: Calculate spread = |MAX - MIN| + ε 
Step 2: Calculate weights = spread / sum(spread)  
Step 3: Calculate direction vector: V = MID - MIN  
Step 4: Calculate sample vector: S = Sample - MIN  
Step 5: Weighted projection:  
        alpha = Σ(weights × S × V) / Σ(weights × V × V)  
Step 6: Clamp alpha to [0, 1]  
Step 7: If alpha <= 1:  
            Predicted = LOW + alpha × (MID - LOW)  
        Else:  
            beta = (alpha - 1) / (1 + ε) 
            Predicted = MID + beta × (HIGH - MID)  
Calibration Data for Mg  
Type  Mg 
Value  Spectrum (21 features)  
MIN  1.2084  1869.31, 439.88, 1608.14, 494.59, 706.97, 1160.62, 545.98, 607.1, 405.16, 111, 45.01, 19, 697.26, 209.56, 75.14, 
51.01, 126, 388.98, 556.899372, 9.015084, 7.350666

METHOD 13: PROJECTION VECTOR  
COMPLETE CALCULATION FOR ZINC (Zn)  
 
INPUT DATA - Sample BR -7 (Sample Index 21)  
Spectral Data (21 features):  
1159.21, 208.78, 998.73, 281.32, 468.92, 923.83, 386.57, 469.43, 338.56, 94.0, 41.0, 15.0, 
572.86, 183.57, 68.98, 46.0, 110.09, 356.73, 86.629, 3.611, 2.644  
Actual Zn Value = 1.04  
 
METHOD 13 FORMULA  
text 
Step 1: Same as Method 7 - Adaptive Projection  
Step 2: Calculate direction vector: V = MID - MIN  
Step 3: Calculate sample vector: S = Sample - MIN  
Step 4: Project sample onto direction vector:  
        alpha = (S · V) / (V · V)  
Step 5: Clamp alpha to [0, 1]  
Step 6: Interpolate: Predicted = LOW + alpha × (HIGH - LOW)

METHOD 14: SPECTRAL ANGLE  
COMPLETE CALCULATION FOR BORON (B)  
 
INPUT DATA - Sample BR -7 (Sample Index 21)  
Spectral Data (21 features):

572.86, 183.57, 68.98, 46.0, 110.09, 356.73, 86.629, 3.611, 2.644  
Actual B Value = 0  
 
METHOD 14 FORMULA  
text 
Step 1: Calculate Spectral Angle between sample and each calibration point  
        cos(θ) = ( A · B) / (||A|| × ||B||)  
        θ = arccos(cos( θ)) 
Step 2: Use angles as distances for IDW  
Step 3: IDW with power = 1.5  
        Predicted = Σ(w_i × Value_i) / Σ(w_i)  
        where w_i = 1/( θ_i + ε)

METHOD 17 FORMULA  
text 
Step 1: Calculate Multi Ratio Score  
        r1 = Band1 / Band2

METHOD 22: BLENDED REGRESSION - COMPLETE STEP -BY-STEP CALCULATION  
COMPLETE CALCULATION FOR POTASSIUM (K)  
 
INPUT DATA - Sample BR -7 (Sample Index 21)  
Spectral Data (21 features):

Actual K Value = 364.74  
 
METHOD 22 FORMULA  
text 
Step 1: Calculate Euclidean distances to MIN, MID, MAX  
Step 2: IDW interpolation (power=1.0) → pred_interp  
Step 3: Calculate Pearson correlations with MIN, MID, MAX  
Step 4: Correlation -weighted regression → pred_reg  
Step 5: Calculate confidence = 1 / (1 + min_distance)  
Step 6: Blend: Predicted = (1 -confidence)×pred_reg + confidence×pred_interp

METHOD 24: PIECEWISE INTERPOLATION  
COMPLETE CALCULATION FOR ZINC (Zn)  
 
INPUT DATA - Sample BR -7 (Sample Index 21)  
Spectral Data (21 features):  
1159.21, 208.78, 998.73, 281.32, 468.92, 923.83, 386.57, 469.43, 338.56, 94.0, 41.0, 15.0, 
572.86, 183.57, 68.98, 46.0, 110.09, 356.73, 86.629, 3.611, 2.644  
Actual Zn Value = 1.04  
 
METHOD 24 FORMULA  
text 
Step 1: Calculate mean values for sample, MIN, MID, MAX  
Step 2: Calculate position of sample between MIN and MAX:  
        position = (sample_mean - min_mean) / (max_mean - min_mean)  
Step 3: Calculate position of MID between MIN and MAX:  
        mid_pos = (mid_mean - min_mean) / (max_mean - min_mean)  
Step 4: Piecewise interpolation:  
        If position <= mid_pos:  
            t = position / mid_pos  
            Predicted = LOW + t × (MID - LOW)  
        Else:  
            t = (position - mid_pos) / (1 - mid_pos)  
            Predicted = MID + t × (HIGH - MID)

METHOD 25: SIMILARITY WEIGHTED INTERPOLATION  
COMPLETE CALCULATION FOR NITROGEN (N)  
 
INPUT DATA - Sample BR -7 (Sample Index 21)  
Spectral Data (21 features):  
1159.21, 208.78, 998.73, 281.32, 468.92, 923.83, 386.57, 469.43, 338.56, 94.0, 41.0, 15.0, 
572.86, 183.57, 68.98, 46.0, 110.09, 356.73, 86.629, 3.611, 2.644  
Actual N Value = 50.4  
 
METHOD 25 FORMULA  
text 
Step 1: For each wavelength i, calculate similarity scores:  
        S_min = 1/(|Sample[i] - MIN[i]| + 1)  
        S_mid = 1/(|Sample[i] - MID[i]| + 1)  
        S_max = 1/(|Sample[i] - MAX[i]| + 1)  
 
Step 2: Sum similarities across all wavelengths:  
        T_min = ΣS_min  
        T_mid = ΣS_mid  
        T_max = ΣS_max  
 
Step 3: Calculate weights:  
        W_min = T_min / (T_min + T_mid + T_max)  
        W_mid = T_mid / (T_min + T_mid + T_max)  
        W_max = T_max / (T_min + T_mid + T_max)  
 
Step 4: Prediction:  
        N = W_min × N_min + W_mid × N_mid + W_max × N_max

LOGIC 3 FORMULA (Without Blank)  
text 
Step 1: Extract selected bands for Ca: [3, 12, 16, 18]  
Step 2: Normalize sample, MIN, MID, MAX by sum:  
        sample_norm = sample_bands / sum(sample_bands)  
        min_norm = min_bands / sum(min_bands)  
        mid_norm = mid_bands / sum(mid_bands)  
        max_norm = max_bands / sum( max_bands)  
Step 3: Calculate Euclidean distances:  
        d_min = ||sample_norm - min_norm||  
        d_mid = ||sample_norm - mid_norm||  
        d_max = ||sample_norm - max_norm||  
Step 4: IDW weights:  
        w_i = 1/(d_i + ε) 
        weights = w / sum(w)  
Step 5: Prediction = weights[0]×min_val + weights[1]×mid_val + weights[2]×max_val  
Step 6: Clamp to [min_val, max_val]

LOGIC 5 FORMULA (Without Blank)  
text 
Step 1: Extract selected bands for S: [0, 1, 17, 18]  
Step 2: Extract matrix bands for S: [0, 1, 17]  
Step 3: Calculate background correction:  
        sample_bg = mean(sample_matrix)  
        mid_bg = mean(mid_matrix)  
Step 4: Correct sample and MID:  
        sample_corr = sample_bands / sample_bg  
        mid_corr = mid_bands / mid_bg  
        min_corr = min_bands / mid_bg  
        max_corr = max_bands / mid_bg  
Step 5: Calculate Euclidean distances:  
        d_min = ||sample_corr - min_corr||  
        d_mid = ||sample_corr - mid_corr||  
        d_max = ||sample_corr - max_corr||  
Step 6: IDW weights:  
        w_i = 1/(d_i + ε) 
        weights = w / sum(w)  
Step 7: Prediction = weights[0]×min_val + weights[1]×mid_val + weights[2]×max_val  
Step 8: Clamp to [min_val, max_val]

STEP 5: Calculate Prediction  
Formula: Prediction = min_val + position × (max_val - min_val)  
min_val = 0.916  
max_val = 366.4  
position = 0.7262  
Prediction = 0.916 + 0.7262 × (366.4 - 0.916)  
Prediction = 266.41

NUTRIENT CLASSIFICATION LOGIC DOCUMENT  
1. WAVELENGTH GROUPING  
Group  Wavelengths  Count  Output  
1 A, B, C, D, E, F, G, H, R  9 ip1 
2 I, S, J, T, U, V, W, K, L  9 ip2 
3 U, V, W (320nm, 280nm, 200nm)  3 ip3 
 
2. FORMULA  
text 
ip = [ Σ(Wi²) / Σ(Wi)] / 100  
Where Wi = each wavelength value in the group  
 
3. INDIVIDUAL FORMULAS  
ip1 (9 wavelengths):  
text 
ip1 = [(A²+B²+C²+D²+E²+F²+G²+H²+R²) / (A+B+C+D+E+F+G+H+R)] / 100  
ip2 (9 wavelengths):  
text 
ip2 = [(I²+S²+J²+T²+U²+V²+W²+K²+L²) / (I+S+J+T+U+V+W+K+L)] / 100  
ip3 (3 wavelengths):  
text 
ip3 = [(U²+V²+W²) / (U+V+W)] / 100  
 
 
Sample ip values:  
text 
ip1 = 11.0223  
--- PDF 2 PAGE 1 ---
ip2 = 3.9093  
ip3 = 5.1995  
 
STEP 5: COMPARE WITH CALIBRATION POINTS  
Assume JSO Calibration Points:  
Point  ip1 ip2 ip3 P 
JSO1  12.2629  4.2285  5.5561  0.916  
JSO2  12.3103  4.1295  6.0681  170 
JSO3  12.3576  4.0305  6.5800  366.4

text 
d1 = √[(11.0223 -12.2629)² + (3.9093 -4.2285)²  + (5.1995 -5.5561)²]  
d1 = √[( -1.2406)² + ( -0.3192)² + ( -0.3566)²]  
d1 = √[1.539 + 0.102 + 0.127]  
d1 = √1.768  
d1 = 1.329  
Distance to JSO2:  
text

d2 = √[( -1.288)² + ( -0.2202)² + ( -0.8686)²]

CALCULATION LOGIC  
1. IPS CALCULATION  
1.1 Formula  
text 
IPS = ( Σ(value²)) / ( Σ(value)) / 100  
1.2 Three IPS Values  
IPS Wavelengths Used  Formula  
IP1 A, B, C, D, E, F, G, H, R  (A²+B²+C²+D²+E²+F²+G²+H²+R²) / (A+B+C+D+E+F+G+H+R) / 100  
IP2 I, S680, J, T, U, V, W, K900, L  (I²+S680²+J²+T²+U²+V²+W²+K900²+L²) / (I+S680+J+T+U+V+W+K900+L) / 100  
IP3 U320, V280, W200  (U320²+V280²+W200²) / (U320+V280+W200) / 100  
1.3 Example Calculation (Sample 1)  
text 
IP1 = (1744.18²+410.44²+...+390.69²) / (1744.18+410.44+...+390.69) / 100 = 7.681876  
IP2 = (106²+43²+...+369.94²) / (106+43+...+369.94) / 100 = 3.486416  
--- PDF 2 PAGE 14 ---
IP3 = (534.78²+8.61²+6.88²) / (534.78+8.61+6.88) / 100 = 0.810108  
 
2. CENTROID CALCULATION  
2.1 Class Assignment  
text 
IF N < 200 → Class = "Low"  
ELSE IF 200 ≤ N < 350 → Class = "Medium"  
ELSE → Class = "High"  
2.2 Centroid Formula  
text 
μ_C = (1/m) × Σ(sample_i)  
where sample_i = [ip1, ip2, ip3] for each sample in class C  
2.3 Centroids (Calculated from Training Data)  
Class  Number of Samples  Centroid [ip1, ip2, ip3]  
Low  22 samples  [8.569986, 3.731463, 1.716387]  
Medium  8 samples  [10.308449, 3.918395, 3.820572]  
High  30 samples  [8.805760, 3.760319, 2.218485]

text

Sample 1: [7.681876, 3.486416, 0.810108]  
Sample 2: [6.96157, 3.525541, 0.450235]  
Sample 3: [7.653761, 3.502305, 0.819216]  
... (22 samples total)  
 
Step 1: Sum all ip1 values = 188.5397  
Step 2: Sum all ip2 values = 82.0922  
--- PDF 2 PAGE 15 ---
Step 3: Sum all ip3 values = 37.7605

      = [8.569986, 3.731463, 1.716387]  
 
3. COVARIANCE MATRIX CALCULATION  
3.1 Formula  
text 
Σ_C = (1/(m -1)) × Σ[(sample_i - μ_C) × (sample_i - μ_C)ᵀ]

text

[-0.888110] × [ -0.888110, -0.245047, -0.906279]  
[-0.245047]  
[-0.906279]  
= [[0.7887, 0.2177, 0.8049],  
   [0.2177, 0.0600, 0.2221],  
   [0.8049, 0.2221, 0.8213]]

Σ = [[76.185, 9.581, 83.844],  
     [9.581, 2.289, 9.013],

5. MAHALANOBIS DISTANCE CALCULATION  
5.1 Formula  
text 
D_C(x) = √((x - μ_C)ᵀ × Σ_C⁻¹ × (x - μ_C)) 
5.2 Step -by-Step Example (Sample 1)  
text 
Sample 1: x = [7.681876, 3.486416, 0.810108]  
 
Step 1: Calculate difference from each centroid  
Low:    x - μ_Low = [7.681876 -8.569986, 3.486416 -3.731463, 0.810108 -1.716387]  
                   = [-0.888110, -0.245047, -0.906279]  
 
Medium: x - μ_Medium = [7.681876 -10.308449, 3.486416 -3.918395, 0.810108 -3.820572]  
                     = [-2.626573, -0.431979, -3.010464]  
 
High:   x - μ_High = [7.681876 -8.805760, 3.486416 -3.760319, 0.810108 -2.218485]  
                   = [-1.123884, -0.273903, -1.408377]  
 
Step 2: For Low Class  
--- PDF 2 PAGE 18 ---
D_Low = √([ -0.888110, -0.245047, -0.906279] × Σ_Low⁻¹ × [ -0.888110, -0.245047, -
0.906279] ᵀ) 
      = √(5.313 × 2.896 + ... + 0.821 × 2.772)  
      = √(15.39)  
      = 3.922 (Example - actual distance = 2.1087)  
 
Step 3: For Medium Class  
D_Medium = √([ -2.626573, -0.431979, -3.010464] × Σ_Medium⁻¹ × [ -2.626573, -0.431979, -
3.010464] ᵀ) 
         = √(34.27)  
         = 5.854 (Example - actual distance = 4.5789)  
 
Step 4: For High Class  
D_High = √([ -1.123884, -0.273903, -1.408377] × Σ_High⁻¹ × [ -1.123884, -0.273903, -
1.408377] ᵀ) 
       = √(7.82)  
       = 2.797 (Example - actual distance = 3.2156)

6. NITROGEN PREDICTION  
6.1 Distance Ranges (from Training Data)  
--- PDF 2 PAGE 19 ---

Medium  1.05  5.22  200 - 350

6.2 Prediction Formula  
text 
position = (distance - min_dist) / (max_dist - min_dist)  
position = clamp(position, 0, 1)  
N_predicted = N_min + position × (N_max - N_min)  
6.3 Example Prediction (Sample 1)  
text 
Sample 1: Distance to Low = 2.1087  
Class = "Low"  
 
position = (2.1087 - 0.15) / (4.85 - 0.15)  
         = 1.9587 / 4.70  
         = 0.4168  
 
N_predicted = 50 + 0.4168 × (200 - 50) 
            = 50 + 62.52  
            = 112.52  
 
Actual N = 50.4  
     Correctly classified as "Low"

7. SUMMARY TABLE  
Step  Calculation  Input  Output  
1 IPS 21 wavelengths  IP1, IP2, IP3  
2 Class Assignment  N value  Low/Medium/High  
3 Centroid  All samples in class  μ = [ ip1, ip2, ip3]  
4 Covariance Matrix  Deviations from centroid  Σ (3×3 matrix)  
5 Inverse Covariance  Σ + regularization  Σ⁻¹ (3×3 matrix)  
6 Mahalanobis Distance  x, μ, Σ⁻¹  D (scalar)  
7 Prediction  D, class ranges  N_predicted  
 
8. KEY FORMULAS  
Formula  Purpose  
IPS = ( Σx²)/( Σx)/100  Calculate spectral indices  
μ = (1/ m)Σx Class centroid  
Σ = (1/( m-1))Σ(x-μ)(x-μ)ᵀ Covariance matrix  
Σ⁻¹ = pinv( Σ + 0.001 I) Inverse covariance  
D = √((x -μ)ᵀΣ⁻¹(x-μ)) Mahalanobis  distance  
N = N_min + ((D -D_min)/(D_max -D_min)) × (N_max -N_min)  Nitrogen prediction  
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
--- PDF 2 PAGE 21 ---
SAM + SRI CLASSIFICATION LOGIC DOCUMENT  
1. INPUT FEATURES  
Feature  Description  Source  
ip1 IPS Group 1 (Visible/NIR)  From 9 wavelengths (A -R) 
ip2 IPS Group 2 (Red -edge/NIR)  From 9 wavelengths (I -L) 
ip3 IPS Group 3 (UV region)  From 3 wavelengths (U320 -W200)  
 
2. SRI (SPECTRAL RATIO INDEX) CALCULATION  
2.1 Formula  
text 
SRI = (ip1 × ip3) / ip2  
2.2 Purpose  
SRI combines all three IPS values into a single index that correlates with Nitrogen content.  
2.3 Example Calculation (Sample 1)  
text 
Sample 1: ip1 = 7.681876, ip2 = 3.486416, ip3 = 0.810108  
SRI = (7.681876 × 0.810108) / 3.486416  
    = 6.2227 / 3.4864  
    = 1.7848  
 
3. SPECTRAL ANGLE MAPPER (SAM)  
3.1 Formula  
text 
Spectral Angle = arccos((v1 · v2) / (|v1| × |v2|)) × (180/ π) 
 
Where:  
v1 = Sample vector [ip1, ip2, ip3]  
--- PDF 2 PAGE 22 ---
v2 = Class centroid [ip1, ip2, ip3]  
3.2 Class Centroids (Hardcoded)  
Class  Centroid Vector [ip1, ip2, ip3]  
Low  [8.569986, 3.731463, 1.716387]  
Medium  [10.308449, 3.918395, 3.820572]  
High  [8.805760, 3.760319, 2.218485]  
3.3 SAM Calculation Example (Sample 1)  
Step 1: Calculate dot products  
text 
Sample 1: v = [7.681876, 3.486416, 0.810108]  
 
Low centroid: c_Low = [8.569986, 3.731463, 1.716387]  
Dot = 7.681876×8.569986 + 3.486416×3.731463 + 0.810108×1.716387  
    = 65.839 + 13.010 + 1.391  
    = 80.240  
 
Medium centroid: c_Med = [10.308449, 3.918395, 3.820572]  
Dot = 7.681876×10.308449 + 3.486416×3.918395 + 0.810108×3.820572  
    = 79.189 + 13.662 + 3.095  
    = 95.946  
 
High centroid: c_High = [8.805760, 3.760319, 2.218485]  
Dot = 7.681876×8.805760 + 3.486416×3.760319 + 0.810108×2.218485  
    = 67.628 + 13.110 + 1.797  
    = 82.535  
Step 2: Calculate magnitudes  
text 
--- PDF 2 PAGE 23 ---
|v| = √(7.681876² + 3.486416² + 0.810108²)  
    = √(59.010 + 12.155 + 0.656)  
    = √71.821  
    = 8.474  
 
|Low| = √(8.569986² + 3.731463² + 1.716387²)  
     = √(73.445 + 13.924 + 2.946)  
     = √90.315  
     = 9.503  
 
|Medium| = √(10.308449² + 3.918395² + 3.820572²)  
       = √(106.264 + 15.354 + 14.597)  
       = √136.215  
       = 11.671  
 
|High| = √(8.805760² + 3.760319² + 2.218485²)  
      = √(77.541 + 14.140 + 4.922)  
      = √96.603  
      = 9.829  
Step 3: Calculate cos( θ) 
text 
cos(θ_Low) = 80.240 / (8.474 × 9.503)  
           = 80.240 / 80.528  
           = 0.9964  
 
cos(θ_Med) = 95.946 / (8.474 × 11.671)  
           = 95.946 / 98.902  
           = 0.9701  
--- PDF 2 PAGE 24 ---
 
cos(θ_High) = 82.535 / (8.474 × 9.829)  
            = 82.535 / 83.297  
            = 0.9909  
Step 4: Calculate angles (degrees)  
text 
θ_Low = arccos(0.9964) × 180/ π = 4.85°  
θ_Med = arccos(0.9701) × 180/ π = 14.03°  
θ_High = arccos(0.9909) × 180/ π = 7.73°  
Interpretation:  Smallest angle = Closest class =  Low  (4.85°)  
 
4. CLASSIFICATION LOGIC  
4.1 Decision Tree  
text 
Step 1: Calculate SRI = (ip1 × ip3) / ip2  
 
Step 2: Check High Class Rules  
    IF SRI < 1.2 AND ip1 < 8.0 AND ip3 < 0.7:  
        → Class = "High"  
        → Use High SRI range (0.1 - 20.0)  
     
    ELSE IF SRI > 8.0:  
        → Class = "High"  
        → Use High SRI range (0.1 - 20.0)  
 
Step 3: Check Medium Range  
    IF 3.0 ≤ SRI ≤ 8.0:  
        → Use SAM to decide between Low and Medium  
--- PDF 2 PAGE 25 ---
        → Class = min angle (Low or Medium)  
 
Step 4: Default (SRI < 3.0)  
    → Use SAM to decide between all classes  
    → Class = min angle (Low, Medium, High)  
    → BUT if SAM predicts High, override to Low/Medium  
4.2 Hardcoded SRI Ranges (with Buffer)  
Class  SRI Range  N Range  Purpose  
Low  0.2 - 4.0 0 - 150 Buffer below 0.5 and above 3.0  
Medium  1.5 - 9.0 150 - 300 Buffer below 2.0 and above 8.0  
High  0.1 - 20.0  300 - 500 Buffer below 0.3 and above 18.0  
4.3 Hardcoded High Rules  
Rule  Condition  Class  
Group A  SRI > 8.0  High  
Group B  SRI < 1.2 AND ip1 < 8.0 AND ip3 < 0.7  High  
 
5. NITROGEN PREDICTION  
5.1 Formula  
text 
position = (SRI - sri_min) / (sri_max - sri_min)  
position = clamp(position, 0, 1)  
N_predicted = n_min + position × (n_max - n_min)  
5.2 Example Calculation (Sample 1)  
text 
Sample 1: Class = Low, SRI = 1.7848  
Low Range: sri_min = 0.2, sri_max = 4.0  
--- PDF 2 PAGE 26 ---
           n_min = 0, n_max = 150  
 
position = (1.7848 - 0.2) / (4.0 - 0.2) 
         = 1.5848 / 3.8  
         = 0.4171  
 
N_predicted = 0 + 0.4171 × (150 - 0) 
            = 62.56  
 
Actual N = 50.4  
     Correct class (Low)  
 
6. CLASSIFICATION SUMMARY  
6.1 Sample 1 Classification Steps  
Step  Calculation  Result  
1 SRI = (7.681876 × 0.810108) / 3.486416  1.7848  
2 Check High Group B: SRI < 1.2?  No (1.7848 > 1.2)  
3 Check High Group A: SRI > 8.0?  No (1.7848 < 8.0)  
4 Check Medium Range: 3.0 ≤ SRI ≤ 8.0?  No (1.7848 < 3.0)  
5 Default: Use SAM  Angles: Low=4.85°, Med=14.03°, High=7.73°  
6 Min angle = Low  Predicted Class = Low  
7 Predict N using Low range  N_predicted = 62.56

9. KEY FORMULAS SUMMARY  
Formula  Purpose  
SRI = (ip1 × ip3) / ip2  Spectral Ratio Index  
θ = arccos((v·c)/(|v|×|c|)) × 180/ π Spectral Angle to centroid  
position = (SRI - min)/(max - min)  Position within class range  
N = n_min + position × (n_max - n_min)  Nitrogen prediction

4. NORMALIZATION LOGIC (Standard Scaler)  
Parameter  Formula  Example  
Mean ( μ) Average of training data  μ₄₁₀ = 1500.0  
Std Dev ( σ) Standard deviation of training data  σ₄₁₀ = 250.0  
Normalization  x_norm = (x - μ) / σ  (1709.45 - 1500) / 250 = 0.8378  
Output Range  Mean = 0, Variance = 1  All features normalized  
 
5. CLASSIFICATION LOGIC (Random Forest)  
Component  Setting  Purpose  
Algorithm  Random Forest  Ensemble learning  
Trees  100 Reduce overfitting  
Max Depth  10 Control complexity  
Min Samples Split  5 Prevent over -splitting  
Class Weight  Balanced  Handle imbalanced data  
Voting Mechanism:  
--- PDF 2 PAGE 38 ---
Tree #  Vote  Count  
1-30 Low 30 
31-50 Med  20 
51-70 Low 20 
71-85 High  15 
86-100 Low 15 
Total  Low=65, Med=20, High=15  Final = Low  
 
6. REGRESSION LOGIC (Ridge Regression)  
Class  Regressor  Formula  Weights  
Low Regressor_Low  y = W₁X₁ + ... + W₂₁X₂₁ + b  21 weights + bias  
Med  Regressor_Med  y = W₁X₁ + ... + W₂₁X₂₁ + b  21 weights + bias  
High  Regressor_High  y = W₁X₁ + ... + W₂₁X₂₁ + b  21 weights + bias  
Example Calculation:  
Feature  Weight (W)  Normalized (X)  Product  
X₁ (410nm)  0.45  0.84  0.378  
X₂ (435nm)  -0.32  -0.12  0.038  
X₃ (460nm)  0.78  0.56  0.437  
... ... ... ... 
X₂₁ (200nm)  0.15  0.33  0.050  
Bias (b)    0.45  
Prediction    Σ Products + b = 0.78  
 
7. SOFT CLAMPING LOGIC (Decision Table)  
--- PDF 2 PAGE 39 ---
Condition  Nutrient Type  Action  Formula  
Value < Min  Normal  Clamp up  min + (0.20 -0.95) × range  
Value < Min  B, Fe, Mn  Soft clamp up  min + (0.05 -0.30) × range  
Value > Max  Normal  Clamp down  min + (0.70 -0.95) × range  
Value > Max  B, Fe, Mn  Soft clamp up  min + (0.50 -0.95) × range  
Within Range  Normal  Proportional  0.10 + 0.80 × normalized  
Within Range  B, Fe, Mn  Proportional  0.10 + 0.85 × normalized
